# Delivery quality

This record tracks delivery defects, review effort, and prevention evidence.
[status.md](status.md) owns milestone state, next work, and owner decisions.
This file owns quality measures; it does not repeat delivery status.

Baseline recorded: 2026-11-04. Sources: all 17 [project lessons](lessons-learned/README.md)
and the local [slice register](status.md#slices). Historical figures are document reports,
not a fresh audit of GitHub review threads. Later entries must name their source and revision.

This file holds the rules, the register and the rows that are not closed. The closed issue rows
and the review rounds of merged PRs are in the [archive](delivery-quality-archive.md) (owner,
2026-11-10). Each count in this file includes the archive rows. To find an old fix, search the
two files.

## 1. Counting rules

| Measure | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Issue | A confirmed actionable defect, contract difference, test gap, or process failure. Keep its type explicit. Suggestions and new feature requests are separate. |
| Unique issues | Count distinct issue IDs after duplicate reports are reconciled. The same defect in several comments or review rounds counts once. |
| New occurrence | A different affected path or a defect introduced by a repair gets a new issue ID. Link its shared cause and, when known, the repair that introduced it. |
| Reopened issue | Reuse the original ID when its repair did not resolve the same defect. Record a reopen event; do not count another unique issue. |
| Severity | Copy the recorded priority, such as P1 or P2, and its source. Use `unknown` when the source does not assign it. |
| Review round | One completed review of a stated revision and scope. Multiple comments in that review are one round. A follow-up review is another round. |
| First-review findings | Unique confirmed issues first found in the first completed independent review. Later discoveries remain separate. |
| Escaped issue | An issue first found after the introducing PR merged. Attribute it to that PR only with evidence; name the discovery PR separately. |
| Fix-induced issue | A new defect caused by a prior repair, supported by a source or reproduction. Link the parent issue or repair. |
| Open issues | Confirmed unique issues whose state is `open` or `partial` at the recorded cutoff. |
| Dropped issue | State `dropped`: a confirmed issue the owner decided not to fix, with the owner's reason and date. It counts in unique issues and not in open issues. |
| Closed issue | State `fixed` with verification evidence, or `withdrawn` with a reason. A commit, merged PR, or applied lesson alone does not prove closure. |
| Unknown | Evidence is absent or incomplete. It is never zero. |
| Zero | A complete review or reconciled ledger explicitly supports no occurrences in the stated scope. |

A lesson can describe several issues; several lessons can describe the same issue.
Lesson counts are source coverage, not defect counts.
Keep estimates and partial review snapshots separate from exact unique totals.
An itemized count is a known subset; it is not the total for a PR or milestone.
Do not add snapshots with uncertain overlap.

## 2. Milestone view

The PR population below comes from the local slice register at baseline.
It includes documentation PRs because they can also have quality findings.
Reconcile the population when a milestone closes or another PR is assigned to it.
`unknown` means the complete issue ledger has not been reconstructed.

| Milestone | PRs in baseline population | Lesson source coverage | Unique issues | Open issues | Escaped issues | Review rounds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M0 | #46, #48, #49, #50 | 6 lessons for #49; other PRs unmeasured | 2 itemized (DQ-310 from #113); total unknown | unknown | unknown | unknown |
| M1 | #51, #52, #53, #54, #55 | 5 lessons for #54; other PRs unmeasured | 6 itemized; total unknown | unknown | unknown | unknown |
| M2 | #20, #56, #57, #58, #59, #66 | 1 lesson for #57; 5 for #59; other PRs unmeasured | 27 itemized; total unknown | 7 known (6 `partial`, 1 `open`); 5 rows `unknown`; total unknown | 10 in the 22 rows that record it; 5 rows `unknown`; total unknown | 7 logged; total unknown |
| Audit 2026-11-05 | #73, #78, #82, #84, #86, #91, #92, #96, #105, #106, #108, #109, #110, #111, #112, #113, #114, #115, #116, #117, #118, #119, #120, #121, #122, #123, #124, #125, #128, #129, #130, #131, #132, #133 (the register rows of this milestone) | no lesson source; written by `pnpm records:counts` | 500 itemized | 59 (59 `open`) | 14 in the 473 rows that record it; 27 rows do not record it | 164 logged |

For a milestone total, count each issue ID assigned to that milestone once.
Count review rounds by distinct PR and round ID.
If any PR lacks full coverage, show the known subtotal with its coverage, or keep the total `unknown`.
Add a row for each later milestone when its first PR enters the record.
`pnpm records:counts --write` writes the Audit row from the rows of the ledger files, and CI
fails when the row is stale (§7, step 5). The notes on the count of each PR that this section held
before #135 are in git history: `git show 5605954:docs/delivery-quality.md`, lines 59 to 84, the
note of #128 in `git show f78f013:docs/delivery-quality.md` (line 83), and the note of #129 in
`git show 79e3a05:docs/delivery-quality.md` (line 84), and the note of #132 in
`git show 1d81188:docs/delivery-quality.md` (line 85).

**M2 close check (2026-11-04).** Population: all five baseline PRs are merged, and #66 is the sixth.
#60, #61, #62, #63 and #64 have no milestone in the slice register, so they are outside it.
M2 has 17 issue IDs, DQ-006 to DQ-021 and DQ-024: 5 `unknown` (DQ-006 to DQ-010), 6 `partial` (DQ-011 to DQ-013, DQ-015 to DQ-017), 3 `fixed` (DQ-014, DQ-020, DQ-024) and 3 `dropped` (DQ-018, DQ-019, DQ-021).
The exact totals stay `unknown`: #20, #56 and #58 are unmeasured, #57 and #59 are partial, the 5 `unknown` rows have no closure evidence, and S4 to S7 are not reconciled.
The numbers in the M2 row are known subtotals. This check did not change the state of a row without new evidence.

**Check of the `partial` rows (2026-11-12).** The owner asked why DQ-011, DQ-012, DQ-013, DQ-015, DQ-016 and DQ-017 are `partial`.
No record states the reason. The builder searched the ledger at `9b0daf7` and at `b32d4c6`, the follow-up rows of `status.md` at `9b0daf7`, and the body and the comments of #59. **Not claimed:** that no reason exists.
What the record shows: `9b0daf7`, the last commit of #59, wrote DQ-011 to DQ-021 as `partial`. This includes DQ-018, which its own row called open.
The [archive](delivery-quality-archive.md#review-rounds-of-merged-prs) has four rounds of #59. Three of them read the working tree after `ec8faa7`. #59 has two commits after `ec8faa7`: `3dc4f58` (skills and docs) and `9b0daf7` (the fixes). No round lists a fixed ID, and the final gate opened DQ-011 again. So no round confirms the fix of any of the six rows.
The builder checks of #66 are the round that lists DQ-014 and DQ-020 as fixed, and the M2 close check above changed those rows.
`pnpm test` passes on `c276665` (2026-11-12). A test that passes is not a round that confirms a fix. So the six rows stay `partial` (owner, 2026-11-12).

## 3. PR register

`Partial` means there is local source evidence but no complete, reconciled issue ledger.
`Unmeasured` means no review counts were backfilled for that PR.
`Ledger` means each finding of the PR has a ledger row. `pnpm records:counts --write` writes its
four count cells from the rows: the rows found by the PR (its discovery PR; any milestone, not
`withdrawn`), those that are `open` or `partial`, those with `escaped: yes` (an issue that the PR
found after an earlier merge let it in), and its rows in the two round tables. CI fails when a cell is stale. Nobody types these cells.
The historical snapshot column gives evidence for a specific review period.

These rows preserve the scope and precision of the source.
They do not add issues to the live ledger.
Source dates refer to the incident record, unless a reviewed revision is given.

## 5. Live issue ledger

Add one row per confirmed issue. Keep stable IDs such as `DQ-001`.
The baseline itemizes ten distinct issues from S2, S3, and S7. These are a partial historical subset.
Their symptoms identify separate defects within each selected source. The other snapshots remain unreconciled.
Do not infer issue severity or closure from a lesson's `applied` status.
This table holds the rows with the state `open`, `partial` or `unknown`, and the rows that a PR
closed after the last move. The other closed rows are in the [archive](delivery-quality-archive.md#closed-issues).
A delivery PR does not move rows: it changes the state of a row here (§7, step 6).

States: `open`, `partial`, `fixed`, `dropped`, `withdrawn`; use `unknown` for historical rows without reconciled closure evidence.
Unknown states prevent an exact open-issue count. The S7 source reports fixes and failing tests, but lacks per-issue verification references.
Severity totals belong to S2 and S7; the sources do not assign each priority to an individual issue.
Record reopen events in the round log with the original issue ID.
For duplicate reports, keep one issue row and link each source.
When backfilling other snapshots, match their reports against these IDs before adding issues.
Keep withdrawn rows for history; exclude them from confirmed issue totals.
Use `unknown` when the introducing PR or related cause is not established.

## 6. Review round log

The reviewer records one row after each completed review. This includes reviews with no findings.
The builder records later fix and verification evidence on the issue rows.
The rounds of merged PRs are in the [archive](delivery-quality-archive.md#review-rounds-of-merged-prs).

Use a local review artifact or an authorized PR comment as the source.
If the head changes after review, the row still describes the original reviewed revision.

## 7. Update and assess

1. At the creation of a PR in a milestone or an audit, add its milestone and source coverage to the register.
   A PR whose findings all get ledger rows has the coverage `Ledger` (§3).
   A PR with no milestone needs no entry (`AGENTS.md` §9; owner, 2026-11-11).
2. After each review, add the round and reconcile its findings with existing issue IDs.
3. After a fix, add the verification result and change the issue state only when evidence supports it.
4. After a new discovery, check whether it escaped a merge, repeats a cause, or came from a repair. Record the general rule and the search of old fixes ([bug fix, step 2](workflows/bug-fix.md)).
5. At PR handoff, after the merge of `origin/main`, run `pnpm records:counts --write`. It writes
   the rows of §2 that it owns and the count cells of each `Ledger` row of the register from the
   rows of the ledger files; CI runs `pnpm records:counts` and fails when a count is stale. Its
   list of ledger files is `LEDGER_FILES` in `scripts/records-counts.ts`: a PR that moves rows to a
   new file adds the file there, and the check fails until it does.
6. At milestone close, confirm the PR population and calculate the totals from distinct IDs in this file and the archive.
   Then move the rows with the state `fixed`, `dropped` or `withdrawn`, and the rounds of merged
   PRs, to the [archive](delivery-quality-archive.md), unchanged, in a PR that changes records only.
   A `withdrawn` row stays out of the confirmed totals there too.
   Do the same move before that time if `wc -c` gives more than 80,000 bytes for this file. If no
   row can move, or if the file is not below 80,000 bytes after a move, ask the owner and do not
   move again (owner, 2026-11-11).
   Exception, one time (owner, 2026-11-13): for the P3 PRs #106 to #112, the limit waits until all
   seven merge. The last of them to merge moves the rows in its record turn, in a commit of its
   own. A script shows that each moved row has no change and that the counts stay the same.
7. Record one prevention action for a recurring cause, with an owner and verification evidence.

The builder maintains this file in the delivery change. The reviewer checks that each row of the
PR is true: its state, commit, test and source. A count that `pnpm records:counts` writes is not a
finding: CI checks it (owner, 2026-11-18, q4 Q3 A).
Quality changes must preserve the scope of existing approvals.

For comparable PRs, inspect first-review findings, repeated causes, fix-induced issues, escaped issues, and review rounds together.
Record the scope and coverage beside any trend. Fewer reported issues can also mean less review.
Do not rank agents or claim improvement from raw counts alone.

A first-review acceptance rate needs a complete denominator: PRs with a completed first independent review.
Its numerator is those PRs with zero confirmed actionable findings in that review.
Show numerator, denominator, period, and missing coverage; leave the rate unknown until the evidence is complete.
